# youtube.com DNS stage in OONI web_connectivity, RU, 3-5 Feb vs 11-13 Feb 2026 — a look, 19 Oct 2026

Question: does the web_connectivity record carry enough about the DNS stage to name the mechanism
behind youtube.com becoming unreachable in Russia on 10 Feb 2026 (reported removal from the NSDI
resolvers; expected symptom NXDOMAIN from the ISP resolver)?

Script: `check_youtube_dns.py` (API client reused from `../T0/pull_api.py`; raw records cached in
`raw/`; rows in `out/<domain>/window_{A,B}.tsv`; full stdout in `run_<domain>.log`, which ends with
two `queries` blocks printed verbatim). Up to 10 measurements per probe_asn per window, the first
10 by start time. No bootstrap, no stage classification, no hygiene filter.

## 1. `domain=youtube.com` as specified: 11 measurements, one probe

The API's `domain` filter matches the input's host exactly. Input `https://youtube.com` is tested
by one probe only (AS60246, PG-19, ooniprobe-cli 3.28.0, test_version 0.4.3): 6 measurements in
A, 5 in B. Its resolver_ip is always Google (74.125.x, 172.217.x) or Cloudflare (104.23.x,
172.68.x), so it never asks an ISP resolver. All 21 queries answered, all dns_consistency
consistent, no NXDOMAIN in either window. This pull cannot speak to the ISP-resolver question.

## 2. `--domain www.youtube.com`: the input probes actually test

| window | listed | fetched (<=10/ASN) | ASNs | msm with NXDOMAIN | probe ASNs with NXDOMAIN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A 11-13 Feb | 628 | 329 | 69 | 13 | AS208397 INFOSTROY (10/10), AS29182 JSC IOT (2/2), AS12714 MegaFon (1 of 4) |
| B 3-5 Feb | 439 | 276 | 67 | 11 | AS208397 (6/10), AS29182 (2/2), AS12714 (2 msm), AS207103 (1 msm) |

The NXDOMAIN rows look the same in both windows: engine `system`, test_version 0.4.2/0.4.3,
`resolver_address` empty, `answers` null, no `rcode`, no `raw_response`, `dns_experiment_failure`
= dns_nxdomain_error, `dns_consistency` inconsistent, `blocking` = "dns", control.dns fine (20
Google addresses). The top-level `resolver_ip` of every NXDOMAIN measurement except MegaFon's own
(212.1.244.99, AS12714) is in 193.232.x/194.226.75.x, origin AS43832 or AS47445, both registered
to MSK-IX (RIPEstat: "RIPN-NS5-RU-MSK" / "RIPN-RU-RND", Join-stock company "Internet Exchange"
MSK-IX). The NSDI service addresses 195.208.4.0/24 and 195.208.5.0/24 are AS41740 "NDNS", also
MSK-IX, and never appear as resolver_ip: OONI's resolver_ip is the resolver's egress address as
seen by the whoami lookup, not the address the probe was configured with. The 195.208.4/5 flag
therefore never fires and is the wrong test for "NSDI resolver" in this record.

Measurements by (resolver egress MSK-IX or not) x (any NXDOMAIN / other failure / ok):

| window | MSK-IX egress: nxdomain / otherfail / ok | other egress: nxdomain / otherfail / ok |
|---|---|---|
| A | 12 / 6 / 24 | 1 / 3 / 283 |
| B | 9 / 3 / 18 | 2 / 4 / 240 |

"otherfail" is android_dns_cache_no_data (Android's wrapper hides the rcode) plus a few timeouts.
The MSK-IX-egress "ok" rows are other probes (TTK-Svyaz AS15774, SPb State Institute of
Technology AS200679, Rostelecom AS12389 ...) whose queries got Google addresses through the same
egress ASNs.

## 3. What this says

* No before/after difference in this sample: the probes that got NXDOMAIN on 11-13 Feb were
  already getting it on 3-5 Feb, from the same egress. Whatever happened on 10 Feb is not visible
  as an onset of NXDOMAIN here. Either it started earlier for these probes, or it hit resolvers
  not represented among the ~70 probe ASNs, or the symptom is not NXDOMAIN.
* The record can show: that the system resolver returned NXDOMAIN while the control resolved; the
  resolver's egress ASN (and so, via the registry, that an MSK-IX-operated resolver was in the
  path). It cannot show: the configured resolver address (empty for engine system in 0.4.x), the
  rcode or AA bit (no raw_response in 0.4.x), whether the NXDOMAIN came from the resolver or was
  injected on the path. Naming the mechanism as "NSDI removal" from this record alone is not
  possible; the 0.5.x fields (T6: raw_response, rcode, udp/doh engines) would be needed and only
  arrive from 17-18 Aug 2026.
* Sample caveats: first-10-by-time per ASN, no dedup by report (AS208397 is a single unattended
  desktop probe reporting repeatedly), mobile ASNs included.

Rate budget: 616 raw fetches with 8 workers, 0 x HTTP 429; `x-ratelimit-remaining` readings were
inconsistent across responses (see raw_api/ratelimit.csv).

## 4. Onset by day (aggregation API)

Script: `onset_by_day.py` (six calls to `/api/v1/aggregation`, RU web_connectivity,
`domain=www.youtube.com`, 1 Dec 2025 - 28 Feb 2026, day grain: no axis_y; axis_y=probe_asn;
axis_y=blocking_type for all probes and with a probe_asn filter for AS208397, AS29182, AS12714;
all accepted, 0 x 429; responses in `raw_api/aggregation/`, table in `out/onset_by_day_www.youtube.com.tsv`,
stdout in `run_onset_by_day.log`). Caveat first: the aggregation API counts the pipeline's
anomaly flag and its `blocking_type` classification, not `dns_nxdomain_error`; blocking_type
"dns" covers every DNS-stage inconsistency (NXDOMAIN, bogus answers, DNS timeouts). Per-ASN
counts are small (AS29182: 1-2 measurements/day; AS208397: one probe with gaps).

The onset for these ASNs is **22-23 Dec 2025**, not 10 Feb 2026. AS208397 had anomalies on 9-10
and 16 Dec that were all http-failure (dns 0), then 18-21 Dec all ok, then 22 Dec 6/6
blocking_type=dns; it is absent until 15-16 Jan (7 msm, 0 anomalies) and from 1 Feb on every
measurement is dns (1 Feb .. 19 Feb, 1-24/day), from 20 Feb its measurements are `failure`
rather than anomaly. AS29182 is 0 anomalies on every day 2-21 Dec and dns from 23 Dec on nearly
every day through 28 Feb. AS12714 MegaFon's early-Dec anomalies are all http-failure (dns 0 until
21 Dec); dns appears 22 Dec (1 of 3), runs at 10-47% of its measurements 23 Dec - 13 Jan, ~100%
14 Jan - 11 Feb, then drops back to 10-37% from 15 Feb when its measurement count rises
(mobile ASN, many probes). The all-RU dns count shows the same step: 0-9/day (0.0-1.4% of
measurements) 1-21 Dec, 14 on 22 Dec, 21-29/day (3-6%) from 23 Dec; the overall anomaly share
(60-80%) does not move, youtube being anomalous at later stages for most RU probes anyway. A
second rise is visible from 12-13 Feb (24, 45, 45/day; 8-14%), the only trace of the 10 Feb date
in these counts, followed by a 5-8x jump in RU measurement volume from 16-17 Feb (2000+/day) at
a dns share of 6-8%. Why 22-23 Dec: not answerable from counts; a raw look at the 22-23 Dec
measurements of these three ASNs (resolver egress, failure string) would be the next step.

## 5. Control: the same day-by-day look for WhatsApp (19 Oct 2026)

Same `onset_by_day.py` (now with `--domain`, a distinct-ASN series, and one extra call per day
with axis_x=probe_asn x axis_y=blocking_type for "distinct ASNs with a dns-classified
measurement that day"), same range 1 Dec 2025 - 28 Feb 2026, same two caveats: (1) the API
counts the pipeline's anomaly flag and blocking_type, not dns_nxdomain_error; (2) daily per-ASN
counts are small and the probe population moves, so shares follow who measured. Outputs:
`out/onset_by_day_<domain>.tsv`, logs `run_onset_<domain>.log`; 379 calls, 0 x 429.

Which WhatsApp inputs RU probes test (measurements listing, windows A and B): `www.whatsapp.com`
is the one with volume (99 / 55 measurements, 39 / 21 ASNs). `whatsapp.com` is the single
AS60246 probe (6 / 5, as for bare youtube.com). `web.whatsapp.com` has nothing in either window
and only 41 measurements in the whole range (15 days, from 23 Dec), so it carries no series.

www.whatsapp.com vs www.youtube.com, "all" series, per period (msm/day, dns-classified/day,
dns share, distinct ASNs measuring/day, distinct ASNs with dns/day):

| period | WhatsApp msm | WA dns | WA share | WA n_asn | WA n_asn_dns | YouTube msm | YT dns | YT share | YT n_asn | YT n_asn_dns |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-14 Dec | 73 | 0.1 | 0.002 | 36 | 0.1 | 583 | 2.9 | 0.005 | 86 | 2.1 |
| 15-21 Dec | 50 | 0.1 | 0.003 | 26 | 0.1 | 490 | 1.3 | 0.003 | 75 | 1.0 |
| 22-28 Dec | 60 | 0.6 | 0.010 | 31 | 0.6 | 544 | 22.4 | 0.041 | 84 | 9.9 |
| 29 Dec-14 Jan | 53 | 0.4 | 0.007 | 27 | 0.2 | 477 | 18.5 | 0.039 | 72 | 8.2 |
| 15-31 Jan | 28 | 4.2 | 0.152 | 19 | 2.9 | 233 | 11.4 | 0.049 | 55 | 6.4 |
| 1-9 Feb | 34 | 3.4 | 0.102 | 20 | 2.8 | 251 | 10.1 | 0.040 | 52 | 5.9 |
| 10-15 Feb | 66 | 14.8 | 0.225 | 34 | 3.7 | 323 | 27.5 | 0.085 | 53 | 5.3 |
| 16-28 Feb | 177 | 19.9 | 0.113 | 88 | 11.2 | 1964 | 130.2 | 0.066 | 237 | 35.7 |

* The 22-23 Dec DNS onset is YouTube-specific. YouTube: dns 0-2/day up to 21 Dec, 14 on 22 Dec
  (8 ASNs), 21 on 23 Dec (13 ASNs). WhatsApp on the same days: dns 1, 1, 0 (0-1 ASNs). What
  WhatsApp does show on 22 Dec is a jump in its anomaly share from ~0.3 to ~0.8, all
  http-failure (20 -> 33 -> 45/day), i.e. a later-stage change on the same date, not DNS.
* WhatsApp's DNS-stage onset is 14 Jan 2026: dns 0/day on 10-13 Jan, then 5, 5, 4, 2, 9, 7 ... /day
  (2-6 ASNs) from 14 Jan on. The same day its tcp_ip count replaces http-failure (13 Jan: 20 tcp_ip
  / 26 http-failure; 14 Jan: 18 / 1), so 14 Jan is a mechanism change for WhatsApp at two stages.
  YouTube shows nothing on 14 Jan (dns 10-20/day before and after).
* 12 Feb is shared, but it is mostly one probe. Both domains step up in dns count on 12 Feb
  (YouTube 8 -> 24 -> 45; WhatsApp 4 -> 21 -> 16 -> 22) while the number of distinct ASNs with dns
  barely moves (YouTube 5 -> 5 -> 5; WhatsApp 3 -> 4 -> 2). AS208397 (the unattended desktop
  probe) goes from 1-5 measurements/day to 15-18/day, all dns, for both domains from 12 Feb.
  Caveat (2) in action: the February step is volume from a few probes, not spread across ASNs.
* The three YouTube-NXDOMAIN ASNs on WhatsApp: AS208397 has dns on 1 of its 24 WhatsApp days
  before 11 Feb (2 Dec), then all-dns from 12 Feb; AS29182 first dns 19 Jan; AS12714 first dns
  9 Feb. For YouTube all three were dns from 22-23 Dec. So for these probes the December
  change was YouTube-only; by mid-February both domains fail at the DNS stage.
* The 16-17 Feb jump in RU measurement volume (5-8x, 2-3x the ASNs) appears for both domains at
  roughly unchanged dns shares; it is a probe-population event, not a blocking event.
* `web.whatsapp.com` (41 msm, 15 days): 38 anomalous, 6 dns, too thin to date anything.
  `whatsapp.com` (AS60246 only, public DNS): 116 msm, 4 dns.

Note on the script: the previous version's probing request (`c.s.get`) bypassed the Client's
3 s pause, so the 379 calls went out at ~2/s; fixed on 19 Oct (the pause is now applied). No
429 was seen either way.

## 6. Raw look at the two onsets: YouTube 22-23 Dec 2025, WhatsApp 14-16 Jan 2026 (19 Oct 2026)

`check_youtube_dns.py` extended with `--asn` (repeatable; removes the per-ASN cap), `--window
NAME SINCE UNTIL` (repeatable) and `--asn-day DAY`; default behaviour unchanged. Columns
report_id, software_name and software_version are in every TSV. Registry lookups via
`T6/ripestat.py`; mobile/fixed-line is `T6/common.py` MOBILE_ASNS. Full output, including the
verbatim `queries` blocks, in `run_A_youtube_dec.log` and `run_B_whatsapp_jan.log`; rows in
`out/www.youtube.com/window_dec_{before,onset}.tsv` and `out/www.whatsapp.com/window_jan_{before,onset}.tsv`.
703 + 38 raw fetches, 0 x HTTP 429.

ASN selection: every probe_asn with a dns-classified measurement on the onset days in the cached
day x probe_asn x blocking_type responses of onset_by_day.py. YouTube: 8 ASNs on 22 Dec, 13 on 23
Dec, union 19 (section 4's "13" was the 23 Dec count alone). WhatsApp: 2, 2, 3 on 14-16 Jan, union 5.

Denominators: www.youtube.com was tested by 80 distinct RU ASNs on 22 Dec (8 dns-classified) and
87 on 23 Dec (13). www.whatsapp.com by 15 on 14 Jan (2), 8 on 15 Jan (2), 21 on 16 Jan (3).

blocks printed: AS31133 (most measurements, mobile; first measurement) and AS5429 (fixed-line;
first measurement); no ASN in this run has a pre-onset/at-onset NXDOMAIN pair to print.

### What the tables say to the three questions (no further interpretation)

1. Is the pipeline's "dns" NXDOMAIN or something else? YouTube onset: 9 of 19 ASNs NXDOMAIN
   (25 measurements), 10 of 19 android_dns_cache_no_data (28 measurements, all Android probes),
   1 an answered Google address flagged inconsistent. WhatsApp onset: 1 of 5 ASNs NXDOMAIN (one
   measurement), 3 of 5 android_dns_cache_no_data, 1 an answered 57.144.245.32 flagged inconsistent.
2. Is the egress the same MSK-IX space as section 3? For the 9 YouTube NXDOMAIN ASNs the at-onset
   egress is MSK-IX (AS43832, AS42139, AS42728, AS47445) in 6 cases, Google in 1 (AS42893), and
   unrecorded (127.0.0.2, resolver_asn AS0) in 1 (AS25515); AS201643's single NXDOMAIN went via
   MSK-IX AS42728. The WhatsApp NXDOMAIN (AS200679) went via MSK-IX AS43832.
3. Did the ISP's forwarding change at onset, or only the answer? Of the 8 YouTube ASNs with a
   pre/at pair: same IP 1 (AS29182); different IP in the same MSK-IX origin ASN 4 (AS12714,
   AS39927, AS57712, AS207103); different origin 3 (AS208397 MSK-IX NS5-RU-MSK -> MSK-IX RU-RND;
   AS42893 own resolver -> Google; AS25515 Rostelecom -> unrecorded). In 6 of the 8 the ASN keeps
   returning answered measurements after the first NXDOMAIN (AS12714 23 of 30, AS39927 21 of 23,
   AS42893 19 of 20, AS57712 6 of 9, AS29182 1 of 3, AS201643 1 of 2); AS208397 and AS207103
   do not (0 of 6, 0 of 2). WhatsApp has no pre/at pair.

## 7. Within-ASN mixture after 22 Dec: probe keys, flips, daily answered fraction, AS42893 (19 Oct 2026)

Output of `mixture_dec.py` (no API calls; reads `out/www.youtube.com/window_dec_{before,onset}.tsv`, regenerated from cache with a `network_type` column from `annotations.network_type`, and `raw/` only for the AS42893 queries blocks). Outcome letters: N nxdomain, D android_no_data, F other_failure, A answered_consistent, I answered_inconsistent; "answered" = A or I; the collapsing rule is `outcome()` in the script. Probe key = (probe_asn, software_name, software_version, network_type, resolver_ip). "Differ" = yes when the non-empty values over the ASN's N measurements and over its answered measurements share none, no when they share one, n/a when either side has none. network_type is present only on Android and iOS records (wifi, mobile, vpn).

## 8. Outcome by resolver egress, www.youtube.com, 19-24 Dec 2025 (19 Oct 2026)

Housekeeping done first: `dnslook.py` now holds the window-TSV contract (columns, path, reader), `outcome()` (moved from mixture_dec.py unchanged; `out/mixture_dec.md` re-generated and diffed: identical), the new `egress_class()`, and the world facts (MSK-IX origin ASNs, public-resolver ASNs, the 127.0.0.2 sentinel, the NSDI ranges) with their provenance; the four scripts import from it and each states its one job in its docstring. `onset_by_day.py` re-run from its cache after the refactor: TSV identical, 0 API calls. Egress classes: msk-ix (resolver_asn in 43832/47445/42139/42728), google, cloudflare, yandex, own-asn (resolver_asn == probe_asn; misses multi-ASN operators), unrecorded (resolver_ip empty or 127.0.0.2, or resolver_asn 0/empty), other. Output of `egress_table.py --domain www.youtube.com` (`out/egress_table_www.youtube.com.md`):

Split of the msk-ix class (19 Oct 2026, after the active measurement in `../nsdi-egress/`): `dnslook.egress_class()` now returns nsdi-measured (MSK-IX origin ASN, resolver_ip in `NSDI_MEASURED_24` = 193.232.230.0/24, 193.232.177.0/24, the egress of a/b.res-nsdi.ru measured from Frankfurt on 19 Oct 2026, `nsdi-egress/out/nsdi-egress-2026-10-19.md`), nsdi-inferred (MSK-IX origin ASN, resolver_ip in `NSDI_INFERRED_24` = the seven other /24s on December NXDOMAIN rows: 193.232.93/139/160/231/236/240.0/24 and 194.226.75.0/24, derived once and written down in dnslook.py), or msk-ix-other (any other MSK-IX resolver_ip; in these windows 194.85.108.60/61 of AS43832 and 193.232.241.50/51 of AS42728). Every other class is unchanged. `egress_table.py` asserts that the union of the three reproduces the previous msk-ix count (`dnslook.PREVIOUS_MSK_IX`: 229 / 3 / 105 for sections 8 / 8b / 9); the regenerated files were diffed against the previous ones first: every row outside the MSK-IX classes identical, and the table-5 rows summed over the three classes reproduce the previous per-(day, probe ASN) rows. Tables 1 and 4 use the union; tables 2, 3 and 5 have the three classes where msk-ix was.

## 9. All-RU egress table, www.youtube.com, 22-23 Dec 2025 (19 Oct 2026)

Fetch: `check_youtube_dns.py --domain www.youtube.com --window dec_allru 2025-12-22T00:00:00 2025-12-24T00:00:00 --no-cap` (the per-ASN cap is now removed by one flag, `--no-cap`, instead of being implied by `--asn`; the section-6 runs correspond to `--asn ... --no-cap`). Listed 1074 measurements from 115 probe ASNs; 840 raw fetches, the rest served from `raw/`; 0 x HTTP 429, but 10-12 s per response against under 1 s earlier. Rows in `out/www.youtube.com/window_dec_allru.tsv` (dnslook TSV contract); log `run_allru_youtube_dec.log`.

Housekeeping: `dnslook.raw()` and `dnslook.queries_json()` replace the three copies of raw-record reading and verbatim-block printing (`out/mixture_dec.md` re-generated and diffed: identical). `egress_table.py` takes `--window`, prints the distinct-ASN denominators first with NXDOMAIN counts, lists the top ten "other" resolver ASNs, and ends with every NXDOMAIN measurement whose egress is not msk-ix (verbatim blocks in `run_egress_<domain>_<window>.log`). Sections 8, 8b and 9 were regenerated together on 19 Oct 2026 after the second pass (N+D column in table 1, table 5 per probe ASN); the unchanged rows were diffed against the previous files first: identical. Output of `egress_table.py --domain www.youtube.com --window dec_allru` (`out/egress_table_www.youtube.com_allru.md`; verbatim blocks of the four strays in `run_egress_www.youtube.com_allru.log`):

Same split as section 8; this run adds `--name-asn AS206385`, which prints table 8 (N+D per day in the three MSK-IX classes side by side, and the class AS206385's rows land in).

## 10. Substitution test: NSDI removal of YouTube (22 Dec 2025) vs SNI throttling of googlevideo.com (19 Oct 2026)

Pre-registered, written 19 Oct 2026 before any data for this section was pulled. Hypothesis (relayed by Kuznetsov): the NSDI removal of YouTube on 22 Dec 2025 substituted for the SNI-based throttling of googlevideo.com because TSPU capacity is limited. Prediction if the hypothesis is true: after 22 Dec 2025, among probes whose DNS still resolves (egress classes other than nsdi-measured and nsdi-inferred, `dnslook.egress_class`), the TLS-stage failure rate on googlevideo inputs falls, i.e. the fraction of measurements whose first failing stage is tls (`stage()` of T1/T0 `extract_stages.py`: https, TLS handshakes recorded, none succeeded; the dns / tcp / tls / http / ok families) is lower in the post window (22 Dec 2025 - 4 Jan 2026) than in the pre window (8 - 21 Dec 2025), by more than the per-ASN bootstrap interval of the difference, while the same fraction for the non-throttled control www.google.com does not move. Prediction if the hypothesis is false: the TLS-failure fraction on googlevideo inputs among DNS-resolving probes is unchanged between the two windows (pre/post difference within its bootstrap interval, and not distinguishable from the control's). Steps: (1) inventory of the YouTube-related inputs in the RU test list, 1 Nov 2025 - 31 Jan 2026, aggregation API only; (2) fetch of the four windows and the shared rules moved into dnslook; (3) `throttle_table.py`, tables only, appended below.

## 11. Post-onset steady window, www.youtube.com, 11-13 Jan 2026, 19 onset ASNs (19 Oct 2026)

Quiet-day check before running, from `out/onset_by_day_www.youtube.com.tsv` and `out/onset_by_day_www.whatsapp.com.tsv` (series `all`): the requested 12-14 Jan carries the WhatsApp onset on 14 Jan (dns_count 0 on 8-13 Jan, then 5 / 5 / 4 on 14-16 Jan and elevated through 22 Jan), while the YouTube series is steady over 8-14 Jan (dns_share 0.025-0.049; 0.040 / 0.032 / 0.029 on 11-13 Jan, 0.035 on 14 Jan). So the window was moved one day earlier to 11-13 Jan, the nearest three days with no event in either series (going later does not help: WhatsApp stays elevated).

Fetch: `check_youtube_dns.py --domain www.youtube.com --window jan_steady 2026-01-11T00:00:00 2026-01-14T00:00:00 --asn ... --no-cap`, the 19 `--asn` values generated from `dnslook.ONSET_ASNS['www.youtube.com']` on the command line. Listed 1379 measurements from 103 ASNs, fetched the 399 of the 19 ASNs (13 of the 19 had measurements) with the usual 8 workers; all 401 responses HTTP 200, 0 x 429; raw_measurement response times min 0.75 s, median 2.44 s, p90 4.38 s, max 7.53 s (against 10-12 s in the section-9 run); `x-ratelimit-remaining` read 2145 on the first response and 0 on the last. Rows in `out/www.youtube.com/window_jan_steady.tsv`, log `run_jan_steady_youtube.log`.

Table: `egress_table.py --domain www.youtube.com --window jan_steady --name-asn AS206385 --compare-window dec_allru` (`out/egress_table_www.youtube.com_jan_steady.md`; strays' verbatim blocks in `run_egress_www.youtube.com_jan_steady.log`). `--compare-window` is new and lives in the function that prints table 8: it repeats table 8's per-day rows for the named window sets beneath, labelled in the day column, so the 22-23 Dec all-RU values sit under the January ones; the section-9 output is unchanged without it (diffed: identical). AS206385 is not among the 19 ASNs, so its column reads none for January.

www.youtube.com, windows ('jan_steady',): 399 measurements, 13 probe ASNs, 2026-01-11 .. 2026-01-13

Step 1 (inventory, `inventory.py`, one `axis_x=input` listing over 1 Nov 2025 - 31 Jan 2026 plus one day x probe_asn call per matching input, 15 responses, 0 x 429, 0.14-6.8 s each; `run_inventory.log`, `out/inventory_2025-11-01_2026-02-01.tsv`): the only googlevideo.com input in the RU list is `https://googlevideo.com`, 40 measurements from one probe ASN, all from 16 Jan 2026, none in either window; no `rr*` or `redirector.` host at all. Inputs with daily volume in both windows: `https://www.youtube.com/` (439-603 msm, 63-91 ASNs per day), `https://m.youtube.com/` and `https://youtu.be/` (20-76 msm per day), `https://i.ytimg.com/vi/JBRf3nEqfZ8/hq720.jpg` and `https://yt3.ggpht.com/a-/...` (7-24 msm per day), control `https://www.google.com/` (8-26 msm, 6-19 ASNs per day). Chosen for step 2 (user's decision, 19 Oct 2026): `https://www.youtube.com/`, so the test is of YouTube's front page over TLS rather than of the googlevideo CDN the hypothesis names; the prediction above is read with that substitution.

Step 2 (fetch): `check_youtube_dns.py --domain www.youtube.com --no-cap --window pre 2025-12-08T00:00:00 2025-12-22T00:00:00 --window post 2025-12-22T00:00:00 2026-01-05T00:00:00`, and the same for `www.google.com`. Every listed measurement carries the one input (`https://www.youtube.com/`, `https://www.google.com/`). Rows in `out/<domain>/window_{pre,post}.tsv`; logs `run_<domain>_prepost.log`. Concurrency as before (8 raw-fetch workers, serial listing): 20 listing pages, 13,681 raw fetches (1,542 of the 15,223 listed served from `raw/`), 13,680 x 200, 1 x 502 (retried), 0 x 429, 0.06 / 2.1 / 4.3 / 93 s min / median / p90 / max per raw response, 79 min in all (13:04-14:23 UTC); the `x-ratelimit-remaining` header read 0 on nearly every response while never refusing.

| domain | window | measurements | probe ASNs | DNS query rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| www.youtube.com | pre 8-21 Dec 2025 | 7209 | 209 | 11602 |
| www.youtube.com | post 22 Dec 2025 - 4 Jan 2026 | 7521 | 225 | 12494 |
| www.google.com | pre | 231 | 49 | 382 |
| www.google.com | post | 262 | 49 | 444 |

Shared rules moved into `dnslook.py` on 19 Oct 2026, each unchanged, each checked by one diff: `stage()` with its helper `kind()` from `T0/extract_stages.py` (T0, T1, T2, T4 were byte-identical copies; all four now import from dnslook; T1 re-extracted from its raw jsonl, the six `derived/stages_*` / `nextmorning_*` files identical to the originals after sorting, the extractor writing rows in worker-completion order; originals restored); the per-unit bootstrap `boot_share()` / `boot_contrast()` with `_boot_ratio()` and the Clopper-Pearson fallback (`clopper`, `_betainc`, `_betacf`, `_beta_q`) from `T6/common.py`, which imports them now (T0 has no bootstrap; T4/common.py and T5b/analyse.py keep their per-input cluster bootstraps, different functions; `T6/jul3.py` re-run, `derived/jul3_connect.csv` and stdout identical); `day_table()` with `counts()` / `split()` from `egress_table.py`, now taking the row classifier and its categories as arguments, egress_table.py calling it with the outcome letter (`out/egress_table_www.youtube.com_allru.md` regenerated, identical). No definition of any of these remains outside dnslook.py.

Step 3 (tables): `throttle_table.py --domain www.youtube.com --control www.google.com --window pre post` (no API calls; stage from the cached raw records with `dnslook.stage()`, stage family = name up to the first underscore so `tls_or_http_unsplit` counts as tls and is shown in brackets; DNS-resolving = egress class not in `dnslook.NSDI_CLASSES`; bootstrap = `dnslook.boot_share` / `boot_contrast` over probe ASNs, 2000 resamples, seed 6; no hygiene, every listed measurement once; output `out/throttle_table_www.youtube.com.md`, run time 7 s):

www.youtube.com (`https://www.youtube.com/`), windows pre + post: 14730 measurements, 298 probe ASNs, 2025-12-08 .. 2026-01-04; stages: tls_timeout 8975, ok 4383, http_timeout 833, dns_fail 328, tls_reset 154, tcp_fail 24, tls_other 17, tls_alert 11, http_reset 2, http_other 1, http_diff 1, tls_or_http_unsplit 1

www.google.com (`https://www.google.com/`), windows pre + post: 493 measurements, 60 probe ASNs, 2025-12-08 .. 2026-01-04; stages: ok 488, dns_fail 3, tls_other 1, tls_timeout 1
